# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_26747394.pdf
Approved as to
form
1y:
ORDINANCE NO.
_____
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE CREATING CHAPTER 12.28, DOWNTOWN GRAFFITI
ABATEMENT PILOT PROGRAM, OF TITLE 12, STREETS, SIDEWALKS
AND PUBLIC PLACES, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AND PROVIDING PROCEDURES FOR THE
OPERATION THEREOF.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That a new Chapter 12.28, Downtown Graffiti Abatement Pilot Program, of
Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, is hereby created as set out in this ordinance.
Chapter 12.28
DOWNTOWN GRAFFITI ABATEMENT PILOT PROGRAM
12.28.010
Creation.
There is hereby created the “Downtown Graffiti Abatement Pilot Program” (Pilot Program).
12.28.020
Definitions.
The following definitions shall apply in the interpretation and enforcement of this chapter:
A.
“Downtown Development Authority” (DDA) means the body corporate created by
the City of Cheyenne pursuant to Chapter 2.76 of the Municipal Code ofthe City of Cheyenne.
B.
“Graffiti” means any unauthorized inscription, word, figure, painting or other
defacement that is written, marked, etched, scratched, sprayed, drawn, painted or engraved on or
otherwise affixed to any surface or public or private property, to the extent that the graffiti was not
given written authorization in advance by the property owner, or, despite written advance
authorization, is otherwise deemed a public nuisance by the Downtown Development Authority.
C.
“Property” means any real property, including structures and fixtures.
D.
‘Property Owner” means a person, real or corporate, partnership or other business
venture owning any property within the boundaries of the downtown development district as set
forth in Section 2.76.040.
E.
“Structure” means any improvement to real property and shall include, but not be
limited to, buildings, walls, doors, utility boxes, sidewalks, windows, fixtures, sheds, storage units,
semi-tractor trailers, mobile storage and masonry.
12.28.030
Prohibited Acts.
It shall be unlawifil for any person to apply graffiti to any natural or man-made surface on
any city-owned property or, without the permission ofthe property owner on any non-city-owned
property.
12.28.040
Program Creation and Opt-Out.
A.
The DDA shall develop and implement the Pilot Program inside the boundaries of
the downtown development district designated in Section 2.76.040.
B.
The Pilot Program shall include at least the following elements:
I.
Written notification sent to all property owners by certified mail ofthe scope
and operation of the Pilot Program;

2.
An option to opt-out of the Pilot Program by responding in writing to the
DDA within thirty (30) days ofthe postmarked date of the DDA’s written notification ofthe
Pilot Program. Any property owner failing to respond to the DDA’s notice as set forth in
this section is presumed to consent to inclusion in the Pilot Program; and
3.
A property owner’s failure to respond to the opt-out notice of the Pilot
Program as set forth above shall be deemed a release ofthe City and DDA from liability for
property damage and personal injury as it relates to graffiti abatement performed in
accordance with the provisions of this chapter.
C.
Written notification to all property owners shall be addressed to the mailing address
of the property owner as identified by the Laramie County Tax Assessor on the Laramie County
Cooperative GIS interactive parcel map site.
12.28.050
Removal of Graffiti by DDA.
A.
Whenever the DDA determines that graffiti exists within the boundaries of the
downtown development district on property which is visible to the public, the DDA may remove
such graffiti to the extent allowed within their annual line item budget.
B.
Graffiti may be removed pursuant to the following provisions:
1.
Use of Public Funds. Whenever the DDA becomes aware or is notified and
determines that graffiti is located on publicly or privately owned property viewable from a
public or quasi-public place, the DDA shall be authorized to use DDA funds for the removal
ofthe graffiti, or for the painting over, power cleaning, or repairing ofthe graffiti, but is not
authorized to provide for the painting or repair ofany more extensive an area than where the
graffiti is located.
2.
Right of Entry on Private Property.
All property located within the
boundaries ofthe downtown development district owned by property owners who fail to opt-
out ofthe Pilot Program as set forth in Section 12.28.030 will be subject to graffiti abatement
by the DDA.
3.
The DDA may only abate graffiti existing on property within the downtown
development district owned by property owners who did not opt-out of the Pilot Program.
4.
No graffiti determined to exist on property owned by property owners who
elect to opt-out of the Pilot Program shall be abated by the DDA.
Section 2.
This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and
publication.
Section 3.
This Pilot Program shall be automatically terminated unless renewed by the
Governing Body prior to November 1, 2020. In the event the Governing Body fails to renew the
Pilot Program, this ordinance shall cease to be of any thither force or effect.
FIRST READING:____________________________
SECOND READING:__________________________
THIRD AND FINAL READING:__________________
Marian J. On, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
Publish: Wyoming Tribune Eagle
2

2.2.2

ENTERTAINMENT AS A LAND USE CATEGORY PERMITTED AS OF 
RIGHT, WITH CONDITIONS, FOR LOT 28 OF BLOCK 2 OF THE 
VILLAGE PUD, ADDING A NEW SECTION 5.11 TO DEFER TO THE 
UDC STANDARDS WHERE THE PUD IS SILENT ON DEVELOPMENT 
STANDARDS, AND, PURSUANT TO SECTION 2.2.1 OF THE UDC, 
AMENDING THE VILLAGE PUD MAP TO REFLECT LOT 28 INSTEAD 
OF LOTS 30 AND 31 OF THE VILLAGE SUBDIVISION FOR THE 
SUBJECT PARCEL, CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED WEST OF 
AND ADJACENT TO POWDERHOUSE ROAD, SOUTH OF OLD TOWN 
LANE).” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. 
That fifty percent of the owners of property, measured by both land area 
and assessed value, lying within The Village Planned Unit Development (“PUD”) 
described herein, have submitted an application to amend to the PUD pursuant to 
Sections 2.2.1, 2.2.2 and 5.6.2.h of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”); 
the PUD was established by the Governing Body by Ordinance No. 3741 on February 12, 
2007.  
Section 2. 
That on January 7, 2019, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the Applicant’s proposed amendments to the 
Village PUD map and text that are the subject of this ordinance, listened to significant 
public comment testifying of their concerns about safety in the neighborhood with the 
proposed indoor entertainment use based on criminal activity taking place at the 
Applicant’s current location, and found that the proposed amendment would not address 
the eight review criteria of 2.2.1.d for the PUD Map Amendment and the fifteen review 
criteria of Section 2.2.2.d for the text amendment, and recommended that the Governing 
Body deny the PUD amendments described herein. 
Section 3. 
That on January 23, 2019, the Public Services Committee of the Governing 
Body of the City of Cheyenne heard the Planning Commission’s recommendation of 
denial and testimony from the Police Chief and the Applicant regarding security 
measures that could be expected to mitigate safety concerns for the neighborhood and the 
Committee recommended that the indoor entertainment use be removed from the Section 
6.1 As-of-Right category and a new Conditional Use category be established for the PUD 
and indoor entertainment be allowed as a Conditional Use for Lot 28. 
Section 4. 
That on January 28, 2019, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne 
considered the Public Services Committee proposed amendment to allow the indoor 
By Alessandra at 10:03 am, Jan 30, 2019

 
 
2
entertainment use of the Wyoming Downs pari-mutuel Off Track Betting wagering 
activity as a conditional use for Lot 28 of the PUD and, instead, voted to allow it as-of-
right with the condition that a site plan be required for such an application and, at a 
minimum, the safety and security measures recommended by the Cheyenne Police Chief 
be established as conditions of approval of the Wyoming Downs establishment on Lot 28 
within The Village PUD. 
Section 5. 
That the Governing Body finds that The Village PUD amendments that are 
the subject of this ordinance do meet the criteria specified in Sections 2.2.1.d and 2.2.2.d 
of the UDC. 
Section 6. 
That, in accordance with Section 2.2.2, Planned Development Plan, Section 
5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, 
of the UDC, the application is hereby approved. 
Section 7. 
That, in accordance with UDC Section 5.6.2, the land use, design and 
development standards and guidelines described herein shall govern this PUD zoning 
classification.  
 
 
Section 7.1. Uses by Right. 
 
 
 
1. 
Block 1, Lot 32, Block 2, Lot 30 and 31; Open Space 
 
 
2. 
Block 1, Lots 1-8, Block 2, Lots 1-16; Townhouses 
 
 
3. 
Block 1, Lots 26-30, Block 2, Lot 27;  
 
 
 
A.  Loft Residential 
 
 
 
B.  Entertainment and recreation facilities and uses 
 
 
 
C.  Fast-Food Restaurant without Drive-Through Window (ITE Manual 
Land Use 933) with two thousand (2,000) square feet or less of gross floor 
area. (or current equivalent).  
 
 
 
D.  General Offices 
 
  
 
E.  Retail and wholesale establishments 
 
 
 
F.  Service Businesses 
 
 
 
G.  Medical Offices 
 
 
4. 
Block 1, Lot 25; 
 
 
 
A.  Loft Residential 
 
 
 
B.  Entertainment and recreation facilities and uses 
 
 
 
C.  Coffee Shop with Drive-Through Window 
 
 
 
 
 
D.  General Offices  
 
 
 
E.   Retail and wholesale establishments with Drive-Through Window 
 
 
 
F.   Service Businesses with Drive-Through Window 
 
 
5. 
Block 2, Lot 28; Restaurant, Indoor Entertainment 
a. In addition to incorporating project design and operation 
measures required to positively address the site plan review 
criteria of UDC Section 2.2.3.d, an indoor entertainment 
application for Lot 28 must also incorporate design and 
operation measures to meet conditions placed on the project by 
the Cheyenne Police Department that address safety and 
security concerns for the site and surrounding neighborhoods. 
 
 
 
6. 
Block 1, Lot 31, Block 2, Lot 29; Parking areas 

 
 
3
 
 
7. 
Block 1, Lots 9-24, Block 2, Lots 17-26 Garages 
 
 
Section 7.2. Minimum Property Area and Maximum Building Coverage. 
 
 
 
A.  Minimum Property Area 
 
 
1.   Townhouse lots as platted 
 
 
2.   Mixed Use Buildings as platted 
 
 
3.   Restaurant Commercial uses as platted  
 
 
4.   Garages lots as platted 
 
 
 
B.  Maximum Building Coverage. 
 
 
Total building and parking area shall not exceed eighty-five (85) percent of the 
entire Village property area. 
 
 
 
Each building not within the common space and parking areas may cover up to 
100% of platted lot. 
 
Section 7.3. Maximum Building Height.  
 
 
 
The maximum building height for mixed use and commercial buildings shall not 
exceed two stories and 50-feet from the foundation to the ridgeline of the roof to 
account for architectural features.  
 
 
 
The maximum building height for townhouses is 30 feet. 
 
 
Section 7.4. Setbacks. 
 
Setbacks for all buildings shall be twenty (20) feet from Sycamore Road, three (3) 
feet from Old Town Lane, ten (10) feet from Powderhouse Road, seven and one 
half (7.5) feet from the north plat boundary line and ten (10) feet from the south 
plat boundary line. 
 
The buffering in this development is provided via the open space located between 
the lot lines and the adjacent city of Cheyenne right of way or adjoining property 
lines. 
 
Setbacks are to foundation lines.  Soffets, canopies, decorative architecture, 
balconies, awnings, emergency access stairs that are designed to match the 
building that they are attached to, covered entrances or support for all of the 
above, will not protrude into the right of way, but can be located in the common 
space.   
 
Section 7.5. Development Performance Standards. 
 
A.  All residential structures and detached garages within The Village will have at 
least 40% concrete siding, decorative concrete block, brick, stone or E.F.I.S., use 
at least two material types and be similar in exterior appearance. All buildings 

 
 
4
shall be constructed in accordance with the current building code of the city of 
Cheyenne in effect during construction. 
 
No two-townhouse units will be identical.  Each townhouse unit will be different 
in one way of each of the following categories:   
1) 
Material to include color and exterior makeup (siding, rock or brick) 
2) 
Structure to include roofline and front porch design.  
 
B.  Mixed use structures within The Village will have at least 40% concrete 
siding, decorative concrete block, brick, stone or E.F.I.S. be similar in exterior 
appearance. All buildings shall be constructed in accordance with the current 
building code of the city of Cheyenne in effect during construction.  
 
The fronts of the buildings and those facing public streets shall include 
architectural features including: 
 
1. 
Repeating architectural features, bays, windows, store front type windows, 
and wall plane changes. 
2. 
Detailing to include entryways, awnings, color changes, brackets and 
decorative wall lighting. 
 
There will be minimum height elevation changes for parapets and cornices of 2’ 
every 40’ or when tenant occupancies change including adjacent buildings. 
 
Each unit exterior of a building will be a distinct color, texture and façade based 
upon tenant space with a maximum distance of 40 feet before a change is 
required. 
 
Elevation and building footprints shall be submitted prior to building permit 
application for mixed use and commercial buildings. 
 
Detached sidewalks will be incorporated throughout the plat with sidewalks 
attaching to roads at corners to facilitate pedestrian crossings. 
 
C.  Common Space and parking areas will incorporate the following. 
 
1.  There will be at a minimum of five public pads within The Village.  The public 
pads will incorporate site furnishings, plantings, bike racks and benches and will 
be an integral part of the buildings within common areas and along Old Town 
Lane.  There will be a minimum of a total of 16 of the above items on the public 
pads within The Village. 
 
There will be decorative lighting in the public pads, parking lots and along Old 
Town Lane for the length of the commercial development and will incorporate 
dark sky principals on the entire development including on the buildings.  This 
includes shielding and design so that no direct lighting leaves the property 
boundaries and poles no higher than 20 feet. 
 

 
 
5
There will be a gateway feature along Old Town Lane to display the development 
name. 
 
Pedestrian crossing will be located at narrowed road sections and pedestrian 
access easements will be continued to north and south boundary property lines of 
The Village to integrate flow between uses. 
 
Transit pick up locations will be between two pedestrian crossings. 
 
Trash containment areas shall be constructed with a decorative concrete block, 
stucco, or E.F.I.S.  There will be a minimum of two commons pace trash 
containment areas.  The restaurant will have its trash containment area within its 
lot boundary. 
 
Grass pavers will connect the southern end of the alley back to Sycamore Road 
along the access easement. 
  
Section 7.6. Landscaping Requirements.  
 
 
A.  Street Tress will be placed along Powderhouse Road, Sycamore Road and Old 
Town Lane on an average of every 30 feet.  Trees along Sycamore Road and Old 
Town Lane will count toward internal landscaping requirements. 
 
B.   Internal Landscaping will be one (1) tree for every 500 feet of 15% of the 
property area not including right of way.  This calculation will require 74 trees. 
 
C.  Up to 50% of the trees can be substituted by five (5) bushes, a boulder, a 
fountain, a picnic area, a bench, a gazebo or a unique design feature as approved 
by the Development Director.  Bushes cannot exceed 30% of the amount of 
required trees and each other item shall not exceed 10% of the required amount of 
trees. 
 
Section 7.7. Fencing Regulations. 
 
There will be no fencing within The Village except along the north and south plat 
boundary, which will provide openings for pedestrian connections.  Boundary 
fencing will be in compliance with City Code Section 17.116.050.  Trash 
containment areas will be screened and match the architectural design of the main 
structures.  Any screening of outside storage within a buildings allowable lot area 
will match the exterior character and detail of the attached building.  
 
Section 7.8. Signage. 
 
Commercial Buildings  
 
Wall Signs - Wall signs are allowed provided the sign does not exceed twenty 
(20) percent of the wall area the business owns.  Wall signs will be integrated into 
the architectural design and color of the development. 
 
Ground or Freestanding Signs – Two freestanding or ground signs will be allowed 
along Powderhouse Road on private property with a maximum height of sixteen 

 
 
6
(16) feet and fifty (50) square feet per side.  All signs must be outside of the site 
distance triangle. 
 
Temporary for sale or for rent signs after final development shall be permitted.  
Each temporary for sale or for rent sign shall not exceed 2’ x 4’ within the 
commercial and townhouse development and must be removed ten days after 
rented or sold. 
 
Section 7.9. Parking.  
 
Each townhouse will have two off street parking spots located within its lot. 
 
Commercial/Residential Units will have at a minimum 160 spaces within the 
parking areas and garages and shall meet the 2006 Road, Street & Site Planning 
Design requirements for parking design and ADA requirements when the 
development site plan is submitted. 
 
Section 7.10. Ownership and Maintenance of Common Area. 
 
Each owner will be a member of The Village Owners Association, which will 
own the common space, and parking areas.  The owners’ association will be 
responsible for management and maintenance of the open space and common 
areas. 
 
 
Section 7.11. Development Standards Incorporated by Reference Herein. 
 
 
 
If the regulations and development standards of this PUD do not address a 
specific development standard or regulation pertaining to a lot or Site within 
the PUD, the most current edition of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code (UDC) shall govern. 
 
Section 8. 
That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone 
change as described in Section 6 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance 
with Section 8 below. 
Section 9. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is 
declared unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 10. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, LOT 4, 
BLOCK 2, SWAN RANCH RAIL PARK THIRD FILING, AND A PORTION 
OF TUNDRA DRIVE, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (LOCATED AT 
THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF TUNDRA DRIVE AND CLEAR CREEK 
PARKWAY).” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. 
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
That Elsey Family Legacy Trust 1, owner of Lot 4, Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail 
Park Third Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming, have petitioned the governing 
body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property described herein and to 
make it a part thereof; and  
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Adam E. Deschler, a Professional Land 
Surveyor, licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 12045; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned PUD 
Planned Unit Development by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 
 
Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

REQUIRED DOCUMENTS: The following documents have been submitted and reviewed by staff: 
 
o 
Master Application; Petition to Annex signed by property owner’s; Annexation Map; Soils 
Report; Signed clearance from the Board of Public Utilities 
 
RECOMMENDATION: Staff recommends approval of the annexation application. 
 
 
According to Wyoming State Statute 15-1-408, annexations are effective upon the publication of the 
ordinance (not the recording of the annexation map); however, most City and County systems only 
update their maps and services upon the recording of the annexation map with the County Clerk. 
Therefore, instead of conditioning approval, Staff recommends that the applicants provide a recordable 
annexation map to the City prior to the approval of the annexation Ordinance on 3rd reading – so that the 
City may record the annexation map as close to the publication of the Ordinance as possible. If the 
recordable annexation map is not provided to Staff prior to 3rd reading, Staff recommends the Governing 
Body postpone approval until the map is provided.   
Through the review process, Staff has identified the following required corrections to the map” 
1. The Legend symbol for “EXISTING CITY OF CHEYENNE LIMITS” does not appear on the map 
itself.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: 
 
 
This annexation application is for one (1) parcel of land at 33.41 acres located at the southwest 
corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek Parkway.  This application was submitted along with a 
zone change application.  The zone change application proposes changing the County Swan 
Ranch PUD and to City Swan Ranch PUD.  
 
Although this land is located just outside the Urban Service Boundary, infrastructure 
improvements such as paved roads and utilities currently exist in this area.  The land is 
designated Industrial on the Future Land Use Map.  The future land uses anticipated in the 
Industrial category are best located with access to major transportation facilities, such as the 
interstate and railroad. Industrial areas are areas intended to provide jobs, services, and 
economic vitality.  PlanCheyenne is supportive of annexing this land into the City. 
 
As with any expansion there will be an impact on service provision within the City.  However, as 
this is a moderately small expansion adjacent to current City Services, the impact on City services 
will be minor.  The property is contiguous to current City limits to the north. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming State 15-1-402, staff 
recommends approval of this annexation.

PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
This annexation is consistent with PlanCheyenne.  
PlanCheyenne encourages the City to grow within 
the Urban Service Boundary and to maximize the 
use of existing infrastructure.  Although this 
property is located just outside the Urban Service 
Boundary, infrastructure improvements such as 
paved roads and utilities currently exist in this 
area.  Multiple adjacent properties have also been 
developed.  Annexation of this property is 
appropriate.  The change from a County zone 
district to a City zone district is encouraged to 
occur concurrently with the annexation to clarify 
standards, allowed uses, and to allow property 
improvements. 
PlanCheyenne encourages future annexations to 
avoid the creation of new county pockets and to 
encourage the reduction of existing county 
pockets.  This annexation does not create any 
new county pockets. 
 
ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS: Before any territory is eligible for annexation, the 
governing body must find that the following criteria are met (W.S. § 15-1-402 (a)): 
 
COMMON WELFARE – An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety, and welfare of 
the persons residing in the area and in the city or town. 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the community.  Being 
within City limits will allow the property to access City sanitation services as well as City Fire and Police 
improving the overall welfare of the community.  
 
PART OF THE CITY – The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would be constituted a 
natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the annexing city or town. 
This property would constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the City. The adjacent 
properties to the north are within City boundaries. The property is located within less than a quarter mile 
of Interstate-25, a designated Highway Corridor on the Future Land Use map.  The property is also 
located near a designated Community/Regional Activity Center on the Future Land Use map establishing 
this parcel as a natural economic and social part of the City.

SERVICE EXTENSION LOGICAL EXPANSION – The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible 
addition to the annexing city or town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available 
to residents of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City services 
normally available to property owners would also be available to this location.  Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation.  Connection to City water and sewer lines is possible upon annexation 
and application to the Board of Public Utilities. 
 
CONTIGUOUS – The area sought to be annexed is 
contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or 
town, or the area meets the requirements of W.S. 
15-1-407. 
The parcel proposed for annexation is 
approximately 20% contiguous to City limits.  
Contiguity is achieved along the northern edge of

is prepared to issue franchises as necessary to 
service the annexed area pursuant to W.S. 15-1-
4102. 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed 
area. 
 
 
CERTIFIED MAILING – The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the 
public hearing required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected 
public utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date, and location of the public hearing required by 
W.S. 15-1-405(a). 
A certified mailing was made to the required property owners and agencies on January 11, 2019. 
The earliest confirmation of receipt was dated January 14, 2019.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_52276565.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: 
“PURSUANT TO SECTION 2.2.1 ZONING MAP AMENDMENTS, SECTION 
5.1.2 
ZONING 
DISTRICTS 
ESTABLISHED, 
AND 
SECTION  
5.1.3 OFFICIAL ZONING MAP, OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE, 
CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM COUNTY SWAN 
RANCH PUD PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT TO CITY SWAN RANCH 
PUD PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT (ORDINANCE NO. 3989) FOR  LOT 
4, BLOCK 2, SWAN RANCH RAIL PARK THIRD FILING, LARAMIE 
COUNTY, WYOMING (AT THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF TUNDRA 
DRIVE AND CLEAR CREEK PARKWAY).” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owners of the property described herein have applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2. That on February 4, 2019, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone change. 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is 
the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned 
application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lot 4, 
Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park Third Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming is hereby changed to City 
Swan Ranch PUD Planned Unit Development (extending the original Swan Ranch PUD Planned Unit 
Development established by Ordinance No. 3989 to the subject property), the Regulating Plan for which 
is attached hereto and incorporated herein as Exhibit A. 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. 
If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval, publication, 
and the recording of the annexation encompassing the area described in Section 4 with the County Clerk 
and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   

Zone Change Application/UDC-19-00007 
 
P a g e | 3 
 
 
This zone change application proposes changing one lot (concurrently proposed to be annexed into the 
City) from County Swan Ranch PUD and County HI Heavy Industrial to City Swan Ranch PUD.  The 
proposed City Swan Ranch PUD is an established PUD for the Swan Ranch Rail Park area and is very 
similar to the standard City HI Heavy Industrial zone district with the exception that industry requiring rail 
service can be accommodated therein.  This property is currently vacant.  The owner has proposed to 
develop the site with a facility that packages and distributes cat litter.  The owner petitioned to annex to 
the City to connect to City water and sewer service. 
 
PlanCheyenne generally considers HI zoning an appropriate zone in areas designated Industrial by the 
Future Land Use Map and this PUD is essentially HI zoning with a few modifications such as reduced lot 
setbacks and alternate landscaping standards. 
 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code, staff recommends approval of this zone change.

ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following 8 criteria of UDC Section 2.2.1.d: 
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this zone 
change.  
PlanCheyenne encourages the City to 
grow within the Urban Service Boundary 
and to maximize the use of existing 
infrastructure.  Although this property is 
located just outside the Urban Service 
Boundary, infrastructure improvements 
such as paved roads and utilities currently 
exist in this area.  Multiple adjacent 
properties have also been developed.  
Annexation of this property is appropriate.  
The change from a County zone district to 
a City zone district is encouraged to occur 
concurrently with the annexation to clarify 
standards, allowed uses, and to allow 
property improvements. 
This area is designated Industrial by the 
Future Land Use Map, and PlanCheyenne 
encourages multiple industrial zones in the Industrial category, including HI Heavy Industrial 
zone.  The proposed PUD is an industrial-style zone and very similar to the HI zone district.  
Examples of primary uses anticipated in this area include distribution and warehouses as well as 
manufacturing and fabrications.  The applicant is proposing to develop this property with a 
facility that packages and distributes cat litter.  The proposed use is compatible with the Future 
Land Use Plan and PlanCheyenne. 
 
2. SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically 
considering existing zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies 
created under the guidance of that plan for these areas.  
The proposed zoning designation is compatible with nearby properties as it matches the City 
Swan Ranch PUD zoning of City land directly to the north.  The proposed zoning designation is 
also compatible with adjacent County land to the west, south, and east which are zoned County 
Swan Ranch PUD and County HI Heavy Industrial.

The Comprehensive Plan designates this area as Industrial.  The proposed City Swan Ranch PUD 
(mimicking the HI Heavy Industrial zone districts) is an appropriate zone for areas designated 
Industrial according to PlanCheyenne. 
 
 
3. ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development according 
to the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject 
property for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district.  
This property is currently in the County 
and is zoned with County Swan Ranch 
PUD and County HI zone.  As this 
property is being annexed into the City, 
a City zone is required for any 
development actions on the property. 
The standards for the proposed City 
Swan Ranch PUD zone are similar to the 
current County Swan Ranch PUD zone 
district. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
4. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
per the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning.  
The applicant is proposing this zone change without proposing any changes to the streets or 
blocks.  This zone change does not negatively affect the ability of this land and the surrounding 
land to meet any of the other standards found in Article 4. 
5. ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY, & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 
According to the UDC (section 5.5.2), the HI zone district is intended for uses of very high 
intensity which should be isolated and buffered in order to protect the community and to ensure 
unimpeded operation of the use.  The City PUD (section 5.6.2) is intended to permit flexibility in 
site and building design in accordance to an approved plan and specific ordinance.  The City 
Swan Ranch Rail PUD is intended to create an area for specialized commercial and industrial

Zone Change Application/UDC-19-00007 
 
P a g e | 8 
 
businesses in a rural setting where rail is the predominant reason businesses are locating to the 
area.   
The proposed development is located in an area that is relatively undeveloped but has been 
designated Industrial on the Future Land Use Map.  The adjacent properties to the south and 
north as well as an adjacent property to the east are vacant.  An additional adjacent property to 
the east has been developed with an office building.  The proposed development is within less 
than a quarter mile of Interstate-25 and is adjacent to the railroad, two key factors in siting 
Industrial areas. 
The proposed City Swan Ranch PUD (which is very similar to the HI zone district) is not required 
to meet any design standards.  Setbacks and lot coverage requirements for the current County 
Swan Ranch PUD zone district are identical to those of the proposed City Swan Ranch PUD zone 
district.  The application has the ability to meet the required setbacks and lot standards 
established by the proposed City Swan Ranch PUD. 
 
6. ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
The applicant is proposing rezoning this parcel concurrently with being annexed into the City.  
The proposed zoning is analogous to the current County zoning.  The applicant is proposing to 
develop this property with a facility that packages and distributes cat litter.  The proposed 
development is able to meet all standards of the proposed zone district.  The City Swan Ranch 
PUD would allow a variety of specialized commercial and industrial uses including, but not limited 
to, impound yards, recycling facilities, or general industrial in the event the specific development 
proposed in association with this zone change application is not executed.  
 
7. REVIEW 
Agencies with comments: 
 
Development: Tawn Hillenbrand 
The proposed zone change is compatible with the area. The large lot area and remote location 
are appropriate for the proposed City Swan Ranch PUD and HI zone districts. (FYI) 
 
The proposed City Swan Ranch PUD is not required to meet any design standards. Setbacks and 
lot coverage requirements for the current County Swan Ranch PUD zone district are identical to 
those of the proposed City Swan Ranch PUD zone district. 
 
Urban Planning: Tawn Hillenbrand 
Although the property is located just outside the Urban Service Boundary, infrastructure 
improvements such as paved roads and utilities currently exist in this area. Annexation of this 
property is appropriate, and a change to a City zone is encouraged to occur concurrently with the 
annexation. This area is designated Industrial by the Future Land Use Map, and PlanCheyenne 
encourages multiple industrial zones in the Industrial category, including HI zone. (FYI)

Zone Change application/UDC-18-00406 
 
P a g e | 3 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application proposes changing two contiguous lots located at the corner of Campstool 
Road and Hutchins Drive from CB Community Business to LI Light Industrial.  This land totals about 4.72 
acres.  This land is currently vacant, and the applicant proposes developing the property with a large 
equipment retailer (specifically tractors) and equipment services facility.   
 
PlanCheyenne generally considers LI zoning an appropriate zone in areas designated Industrial by the 
Future Land Use Map.  The LI zone is compatible with the surrounding area and the proposed 
development on the parcels in question.  
 
The LI zone district is intended for intensive employment uses that may have accessory commercial, 
service or office uses, but cannot readily fit into other general business or mixed-use areas due to the 
nature or intensity of operations.  Uses in the LI zone district often have impacts such as noise, outdoor 
storage, and freight needs that make them incompatible in other areas of the community.  The proposed 
development as well as the current commercial development in this area match the development patterns 
anticipated by areas zoned LI. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code, staff recommends approval of this zone change.

identified in the Comprehensive Plan.

zone change.

zone.  Examples of primary uses

CB Community Business zoning 
district.  The proposed zone district is 
LI Light Industrial.  The applicant is 
proposing to develop this property 
with a large equipment (specifically 
tractors) retailer and equipment 
service facility.  Setbacks and lot 
coverage requirements for the current 
zone district are identical to those of 
the proposed zone district.    
Changing the zone district from CB to 
LI would remove building design 
standards allowing for more flexibility 
when designing the proposed 
structure.  Lot access width standards 
for the LI zone district allow 
significantly wider accesses than the 
access width standards established for 
the CB zone district.  The wider accesses widths allowed in the LI zone would be more 
compatible with the access widths allowed and/or built on neighboring properties and to 
accommodate the larger vehicles and trailers for the Applicant’s proposed use. 
 
4. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
per the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning.  
The applicant is proposing this zone change without proposing any changes to the streets or 
blocks.  This zone change does not negatively affect the ability of this land and the surrounding 
land to meet any of the other standards found in Article 4. 
 
5. ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY, & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 
According to the UDC (section 5.5.1), the LI zone district is intended for environmentally 
controlled manufacturing or intensive employment uses that may have accessory commercial, 
service or office uses, but that cannot easily fit into other general business or mixed-use areas 
due to the nature of the operation.   
SUBJECT 
PROPERTY 
HI 
HI 
LI 
CB 
CB 
CB 
LI 
AG 
Map #4 – Current Zone Districts 
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS Cooperative Program 
Zone Districts 

Zone Change application/UDC-18-00406 
 
P a g e | 8 
 
The proposed development is located in an area that is relatively undeveloped but has been 
designated Industrial on the Future Land Use Map.  The adjacent property to the south is a large 
equipment retailer with outdoor sales, similar to the proposed project.  The property to the east 
is also a large equipment retailer while the properties to the west and north remain vacant.  The 
property is within a half mile of Interstate-80 and within a short distance to the railroad, two 
important factors in siting industrial areas. 
The proposed LI zone district is not required to meet any design standards.  Setbacks and lot 
coverage requirements for the current CB zone district are identical to those of the proposed LI 
zone district.  The application has the ability to meet the required setbacks and lot standards 
established by the proposed LI zone. 
 
6. ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
The applicant is proposing rezoning this land with the intent to develop the property with a large 
equipment retailer (specifically tractors) and equipment services facility which includes outdoor 
sales.  The applicant is pursuing a Conditional Use Application for outdoor sales concurrently with 
this zone change application.  The proposed development is able to meet all standards of the 
proposed zone district.  The LI zone would allow a variety of commercial and low-intensity 
industrial uses including but not limited to office space, general retail, grocery store, or artist 
studios in the event the specific development proposed in association with this zone change 
application is not executed. 
 
 7. REVIEW 
Agencies with comments: 
 
Development: Tawn Hillenbrand 
Setbacks and lot coverage requirements do not change with this zone change.  Changing from 
CB to LI removes building design standards.  Industrial zoning allows for wider lot access making 
the proposed LI zone district more suitable for the proposed development. (FYI) 
 
Urban Planning: Tawn Hillenbrand 
This area is designated Industrial on the Future Land Use Plan in PlanCheyenne.  LI zoning is an 
appropriate zone designation for this area.  Surrounding zoning includes HI and LI; the proposed 
LI zone district is compatible with these districts. (FYI) 
 
Development: Susana Montana 
Note that outdoor storage is a conditional use in the LI zone and Limited Outdoor Storage is a 
principle use.  Either outdoor storage requires screening per UDC 5.8.6. P&D STAFF: Provided as 
FYI. 
 
Reviewing agencies without comments: 
Engineering Department: Amy Allen 

Zone Change application/UDC-18-00406 
 
P a g e | 9 
 
Engineering Department: Molly Bennett 
GIS/Engineering/Addressing: Gary Stogsdill  
Metro Planning Office (MPO): Nancy Olson  
 
Reviewing agencies without response: 
Parks and Recreation Admin: Sam Payne 
 
Other Comments: 
 
None 
 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or 
general welfare. 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not harm the public health, safety, or 
welfare of the public.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_88985824.pdf
Approved as to
/ronIy
,,j
Resolution
A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF A FEDERAL MINERAL ROYALTY
CAPITAL CONSTRUCTION ACCOUNT GRANT APPLICATION TO THE STATE LOAN
AND INVESTMENT BOARD ON BEHALF OF THE GOVERNING BODY FOR THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE FOR THE PURPOSE OF FUNDING THE FINISH OF THE SECOND
FLOOR OF A NEW MUNICIPAL COURTHOUSE.
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the Federal
Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account Grant program to assist in fmancing this project;
and
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne recognizes the need for the project;
and
WHEREAS, the Federal Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account Grant program requires
that certain criteria be met, as described in the State Loan and Investment Boards Rules and
Regulations governing the program, and to the best of our knowledge this application meets
those criteria; and
WHEREAS, the project cost is estimated at $1,991,200 and the Governing Body ofthe City of
Cheyenne plans to provide the 50% match ($995,600) required for the Federal Mineral Royalty
Capital Construction Account Grant from City Reserves and the status of these matching funds
will be approved with the approval of this resolution.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY FOR THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE, that a grant application in the amount of $995,600 be submitted to the
State Loan and Investment Board for consideration at the June 20, 2019 meeting to assist in
funding the finish of the second floor of a new municipal courthouse, and that the City of
Cheyenne commit the same for this project.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, is hereby designated
as the authorized representative ofthe City of Cheyenne, to act on behalf of the Governing Body
on all matters related to this grant application.
PASSED, APPROVED AND ADOPTED THIS l’ day of February 2019.
Marian I. Off, Mayor
Attest:
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

C Solutions
Owner’s Representative
Ben Hornok
January22, 2019
Cindy Schneider
Grants Manager
City of Cheyenne
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
Re: New Municipal Courthouse Second-Floor Budget
Dear Cindy,
Attached to this letter you will find a budget estimate for the completion of the second-floor area in the new
Municipal Courthouse. These budget numbers were based on information obtained from the Architect and the
General Contractor as we have developed project construction estimates during the ongoing design phase.
The additional cost to the project to finish the second-floor is estimated to be $1,991,200.
The total cost to complete second floor is around $2.6M but items that you have noted that cannot be included
in the grant application have been deleted out such as furniture and contingency reserve. This estimate is also
based on second-floor completion at the same time as the initial construction of the building, utilizing the same
design and construction teams that will be in place. This allows for efficiencies, potential cost savings, and
greater value to the City for this investment.
Please let me know if you should have any questions.
Respectfully,
Ben Hornok, PMP
Owner
T C Solutions, LLC
T C Solutions, LLC
Cheyenne, WY $2009
(307) 274-5569
benhomok@tcsolutions365.com

.TC Solutions
Second-floor Cost Estimate:
City of Cheyenne Administrative Expenses
$434,535.00
Design Fee
$95,700.00
Construction Costs
$1,460,965.00
Total:
$1,991,200.00
Note: These costs are based on a construction schedule coinciding with the current construction of the building
and utilizing the same design and construction teams. Cost estimates are also based on a second-floor usage of
general office and administrative function for the City of Cheyenne. Additional costs would be incurred if the
usage would be different such as including a server room for the iT department.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_76274016.pdf
RESOLUTION NO._______
ApproVec
ENTITLED:
“A
RESOLUTION
AUTHORIZING
THE
CHEYENNE
POLICE
DEPARTMENT TO APPLY FOR OFFICE ON VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN
OVW-2019-15743
IMPROVING
CRIMINAL
JUSTICE
RESPONSES
TO
DOMESTIC VIOLENCE, SEXUAL ASSAULT, AND STALKING GRANT
PROGRAM CFDA NUMBER 16.590-GRANTS TO ENCOURAGE ARRESTS
POLICIES AND ENFORCEMENT OF PROTECTION ORDERS PROGRAM.
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program wishes to apply
for grant funds for reimbursement of expenses related to the Cheyenne Police Department
Community Advocacy Response Initiative Unit (CARl Unit).
The related expenses include:
overtime for domestic violence/sexual assault patrol units and detective position, prosecution,
victim advocacy, personnel, contract training, internal training, internal expenses, internal
supplies, related to the Cheyenne Police Department Community Advocacy Response Initiative
Unit (CAM Unit); and
WFIEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program, will submit an
application for grant funding through the United States Department of Justice, Office on Violence
Against Women (OVW) (www.ovw.usdoi.gov) and www.grants.gov; and
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program will apply for
the grant using DUNS # 026908405; and
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program activities for the
program scope and purpose will be supported by the Arrest Program are determined by statute,
federal regulations, and OVW policies. The project is bound by the provisions of the solicitation,
the Financial Guide, and the conditions of the recipient award if awarded; and
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program acknowledges
that it will assume a 36 month obligation with respect to the Cheyenne Police Department
Community Advocacy Response Initiative Unit (CAM Unit).
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program
is hereby authorized to apply for grant funding for the Cheyenne Police Department Community
Advocacy Response Initiative Unit (CAM Unit) under the United States Department of Justice,
Office on Violence Against Women (OVW) (www.ovw.usdoi.gov) Fiscal Year 2019 (October 1,
2018 to October 1, 2022).
BE if FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor and City Clerk are authorized to execute any and
all documents, including a grant agreement, to secure grant funding under the United States
Department of Justice, Office on Violence Against Women (OVW) (www.ovw.usdoj.gov)
Fiscal Year 2019 under OVW-2019-15743 Grants to Encourage Arrest Policies and Enforcement
of Protections Orders Program.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_42592506.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF A CAPITAL 
CONSTRUCTION LOAN APPLICATION TO THE STATE LOAN AND INVESTMENT 
BOARD ON BEHALF OF THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE TO 
FUND THE REPLACEMENT OF FIRE STATION #3.” 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the Capital 
Construction Loan program to assist in financing the replacement of Fire Station #3 in Cheyenne, 
Wyoming; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne recognizes the need for a new Fire 
Station #3; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne agrees to maintain Fire Station #3 for 
the life of the loan; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Capital Construction Loan program requires that certain criteria be met, as 
described in the State Loan and Investment Boards Rules and Regulations governing the program, 
and to the best of our knowledge, this application meets those criteria; and 
 
WHEREAS, the estimated cost for the project is $5,097,485.00 and the City of Cheyenne would 
like to borrow this full amount; and 
 
WHEREAS, the State of Wyoming will hold the mortgage for the land and any buildings added 
to that land as loan security.  The City of Cheyenne anticipates it will use future 6th Penny Sales 
Tax revenues or will use monies from the general fund for the loan repayment.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY FOR THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, that a loan application in the amount of $5,097,485.00 be submitted to the State 
Loan and Investment Board for consideration at the June 6, 2019 meeting, to replace Fire Station 
#3.  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, and Ron Downey, City 
Treasurer, are hereby designated as the authorized representatives of the City of Cheyenne, to act 
on behalf of the Governing Body on all matters related to this grant application. 
 
PASSED, APPROVED AND ADOPTED THIS 25th day of February, 2019. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
___________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Marian J. Orr, Mayor 
 
 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
___________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones , City Clerk 
By Alessandra at 2:21 pm, Feb 04, 2019

Chapter 38 Capital Construction Loan: Replacement of Fire Station #3 
 
 
 
Fire Station #3 is located at 1720 Cleveland Avenue and serves the east side of the city.  The Fire 
Station is 38 years old, constructed in 1981.  The station has a significant number of items that 
need to be addressed, from the structural to the design layout. 
 
The existing station has no additional space to expand the services of additional crews in the future.  
Multiple roof leaks have been an on-going problem for years without successful repairs to stress 
cracks in the structural walls.  For today’s firefighting, the station design and layout is inadequate.  
In addition, there are a multitude of other issues.  
 
In 2017, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Chief Greg Hoggatt directed his staff to conduct a thorough 
examination of each fire station and to submit a report to him.    The chief condensed the report 
and added his conclusions and recommendation into an executive summary in October 2017.  
Regarding Fire Station #3, Chief Hoggatt states, “The station should be considered for replacement 
in the very near future.” 
 
If the Capital Construction Loan is approved, the State of Wyoming will hold the mortgage for the 
land and any buildings added to that land, as loan security.  The City of Cheyenne anticipates it 
will use future 6th Penny Sales Tax revenues or will use monies from the general fund for the loan 
repayment. 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_23223946.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF A CAPITAL 
CONSTRUCTION LOAN APPLICATION TO THE STATE LOAN AND INVESTMENT 
BOARD ON BEHALF OF THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
FOR THE PURPOSE OF FUNDING THE REPLACEMENT OF FIRE STATION #5.”  
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the Capital 
Construction Loan program to assist in financing the replacement of Fire Station #5 in Cheyenne, 
Wyoming; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne recognizes the need for a new Fire 
Station #5; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne agrees to maintain Fire Station #5 for 
the life of the loan; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Capital Construction Loan program requires that certain criteria be met, as 
described in the State Loan and Investment Boards Rules and Regulations governing the program, 
and to the best of our knowledge, this application meets those criteria; and 
 
WHEREAS, the estimated cost for the project is $5,097,485.00 and the City of Cheyenne would 
like to borrow this full amount; and 
 
WHEREAS, the State of Wyoming will hold the mortgage for the land and any buildings added 
to that land as loan security.  The City of Cheyenne anticipates it will use future 6th Penny Sales 
Tax revenues or will use monies from the general fund for the loan repayment.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY FOR THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, that a loan application in the amount of $5,097,485.00 be submitted to the State 
Loan and Investment Board for consideration at the June 6, 2019 meeting to replace Fire Station 
#5.  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, and Ron Downey, City 
Treasurer, are hereby designated as the authorized representatives of the City of Cheyenne, to act 
on behalf of the Governing Body on all matters related to this grant application. 
 
PASSED, APPROVED AND ADOPTED THIS 25th day of February, 2019. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
___________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Marian J. Orr, Mayor 
 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
___________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
By Alessandra at 2:20 pm, Feb 04, 2019

Chapter 38 Capital Construction Loan: Replacement of Fire Station #5 
 
 
 
Fire Station #5 is in North Cheyenne and serves the Dell Range Blvd. commercial corridor.  The 
Fire Station is 56 years old (constructed in 1963) and has numerous problems, as it is no longer 
able to adequately serve Cheyenne’s citizens.  There has been expansive growth in North 
Cheyenne’s commercial occupancies.   Residential growth to the north and east of the old station 
has also exploded in the past 56 years, rendering the existing station inadequate to ensure proper 
coverage to both commercial and residential areas. 
 
The existing station is too small to accommodate more than three firefighters.  The very populated 
service area of Cheyenne requires more firefighters than the three that can currently be housed in 
the small old station.  Replacing Station #5 with a larger station will address this issue.  Further, 
problems from sheer age and wear have found this station unfit for continued service. 
 
The 56-year-old Fire Station was not built to accommodate the numerous advances in firefighting, 
equipment and technology that now exist. A new station, to replace Station #5, will provide for 
the care of firefighters, vehicles, and equipment.  It will be built to provide all that is needed for 
firefighting in 2019 and for many, many years to come. 
 
Given the poor condition of Fire Station #5, Cheyenne’s Fire Chief, Greg Hoggatt, noted “the only 
repairs that should be authorized are those that keep the station operational until the new station 
can be built.” 
 
In 2017, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Chief Greg Hoggatt directed his staff to conduct a thorough 
examination of each fire station and to submit a report to him.    The chief condensed the report 
and added his conclusions and recommendation into an executive summary in October 2017.  
Regarding Fire Station #5, Chief Hoggatt states, “Begin the process to replace the station that will 
meet the needs of the department in the future.” 
 
If the Capital Construction Loan is approved, the State of Wyoming will hold the mortgage for the 
land and any buildings added to that land, as loan security.  The City of Cheyenne anticipates it 
will use future 6th Penny Sales Tax revenues or will use monies from the general fund for the loan 
repayment. 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_64540258.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION DIRECTING 
THE AVAILABILITY 
OF 
THE 
PRODUCTION OF TCHAIKOVSKY’S THE NUTCRACKER BALLET AT 
THE CHEYENNE CIVIC CENTER.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, attending Tchaikovsky’s The Nutcracker Ballet is holiday tradition for 
many Laramie County residents; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of City of Cheyenne wishes to express its support for 
Laramie County based production companies and Laramie County performers; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body believes this Resolution will afford Laramie County 
based production companies and Laramie County performers an opportunity to collaborate and 
put forth a production which emphasizes local involvement; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, nothing in this Resolution is intended to alter or change the current policies 
and practices of the Cheyenne Civic Center with regards to reservations and confirmations for 
any production other than Tchikovsky’s The Nutcracker Ballet and only during the month of 
December. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the production of Tchaikovsky’s The Nutcracker 
Ballet shall be open to Laramie County based production companies only, on years ending with 
an even number, and to any production company on years ending with an odd number. 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that if no Laramie County based production company 
has confirmed the production of Tchaikovsky’s The Nutcracker Ballet by February 28th of any 
even numbered year, that the availability to perform this ballet will be opened to any production 
company.  
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2019. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Marian J. Orr, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---

Approved as to
OCHS LAW FIR
TRIAL LAWYERS
LEGAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
RE:
Civil suit against those parties legally responsible for the wrongful
distribution of prescription opiates and the deleterious effects it has had on the
City of Cheyenne for which such parties have collectively been fined and paid
over $500 million in civil penalties.
The City of Cheyenne (hereinafter “CLIEt-4’T”) hereby retains outside counsel,
pursuant to the Rules of Professional Responsibility on a contingent fee basis, to pursue j
civil remedies against those in the chain of distribution of prescription opiates responsible for
the opioid epidemic which is plaguing the City of Cheyenne including, but not limited to,
filing a claim for public nuisance to abate the damages
caused thereby. Jason E. Ochs, of
the Ochs Law Firm, shall serve as CO-LEAD COUNSEL together with Steven J. Skikos and
Mark Crawford of Skikos Crawford (whom
are appointed as Opioid MDL National Liaison
Counsel).
In consideration, CLIENT agrees to pay twenty percent (20%) of the total recovery
(gross) as an attorney fee whether the claim is resolved by compromise, settlement, or trial
and verdict (and appeal). The CLIENT acknowledges this fee is reasonable given the time and
labor required, the novelty and difficulty of the questions involved, and the skill requisite to
perform the legal service properly,
the likelihood this employment will preclude other
employment by the lawyer(s), the fee customarily charged in the locality for similar legal
services, the anticipated (contingent) litigation expenses and the anticipated results obtained,
the experience, reputation, and ability of the lawyer or lawyers performing the services and
the fact that the fee is contingent upon a successful recovery. There is no fee if there is no
recovery.
This fee applies to any sums, benefit or value obtained on your behalf, whether monetary
or non-monetary, including equitable remedies including abatement, and whether recovered
by award, judgment, settlement, bankruptcy proceedings, government-imposed reparations
proceedings,
regulatory relief/settlement, non-monetary remedial benefit provided
by
defendants or otherwise. Gross recovery is the value, at the time of settlement or collection
(whichever confers the greater value), of any sums, benefit or value obtained on your behalf,
as set out above. The gross recovery shall be calculated on the amount obtained before the
deduction of costs and expenses. CLIENT grants Attorneys an interest in a fee based on the
gross recovery. If a court awards attorneys’ fees, Attorneys shall receive the greater of “the
gross recovery-based contingent fee” or the attorneys’ fees awarded.

The law firms agree to advance all necessary litigation expenses necessary to prosecute
these claims, and the CLIENT shall have no obligation to advance any costs or to make any
financial contribution to the lawsuit while the litigation is pending.
All such litigation
expenses, including the reasonable internal costs of electronically stored information (ESI)
and electronic discovery generally, or the direct costs incurred from any outside contractor
for those services, will be deducted from any recovery after the contingent fee is calculated.
There is no reimbursement of litigation expenses if there is no recovery and CO-LEAD
COUNSEL further agree to limit case costs to no more than fifteen yercent (15%) of any
gross recovery; thereby providing a net recovery of a minimum of sixty-five percent
(65%) to CLIENT in the event of a successful resolution.
Funds that are recovered on behalf of CLIENT shall flow through the CLIENT and may
be expended on education, law enforcement and jails, healthcare, or in any other way that the
CLIENT deems appropriate.
CO-LEAD COUNSEL shall act as a contact person to keep the CLIENT reasonably
informed about the status of the matter in a manner deemed appropriate by the CLIENT and
the Rule of Professional Conduct I .4. The CLIENT at all times shall retain the authority to
decide the disposition of the case and maintain absolute control of the litigation in accordance
with the Rules of Professional Conduct 1.2.
Upon conclusion ofthis matter, CO-LEAD COUNSEL shall provide the CLIENT with a
written statement stating the outcome of the matter and, if there is a recovery, showing the
remittance to the client and the method of its determination as required by Rule 1.5(c) of the
Rules of Professional Conduct. The closing statement shall specii’ the manner in which the
compensation was determined under the agreement, any costs and expenses deducted by the
lawyer from the judgment or settlement involved, and, ifapplicable, the actual division ofthe
lawyers’ fees with a lawyer not in the same firm, as contemplated by Rule l.5(e)(2) of the
Wyoming Rules of Professional Conduct.
Nothing in this Agreement and nothing in the Attorneys’ statement to the CLIENT may be
construed as a promise or guarantee about the outcome of this matter. The Attorneys make no such
promises or guarantees. Attorneys’ comments about the outcome of this matter are expressions of
opinion only and the Attorneys make no guarantee as to the outcome of any litigation, settlement
or trial proceedings.

day of
, 2018.

The National Opioid Epidemic:
Legal Action by Counties and Cities
to Abate the Opioid Epidemic and Recover Costs to Communities
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
The opioid epidemic is now a nationally recognized crisis. Its causes are coming to light: intentional
opioid overprescribing fueled by overpromotion and the failure of manufacturers and distributors to
perform their legal duty to monitor suspicious orders to warn of the increased abuse risk of new
formulations.
The consequences of the opioid epidemic transcend party lines, socioeconomic lines and racial
lines. Opioid abuse is now the leading cause of death for those under age SO. Many of the costs of the
epidemic have fallen on financially burdened counties, cities and municipalities. The epidemic is
creating pressure on healthcare facilities, law enforcement agencies, first responders and the
communities at large, resulting in rising costs, a strain on resources, and urgent concerns about public
safety. It has only grown worse as people who were addicted to prescription pills have, thanks to
heightened law enforcement efforts that limit supply, turned to street opioids like heroin.
Counties and cities are taking action now to file legal actions against prescription opioid
manufacturers and distributors to hold them accountable for the current crisis. This is not a class
action—in order for counties and cities to participate to assure they have a voice in the litigation and
in any resolution, they must file their own actions.
The primary goal of the litigation is to recover not only tax dollars spent on the epidemic in the
past, but also to secure funds for programs that can effectively treat and reverse this epidemic. These
may include:
P
Awareness/Education Campaigns
P
Preventative Measures
P
Monitoring Prescriptions and Use
P
Emergency Costs/Naloxone Overdose Kits
P
Treatment Programs
P
Disposal Programs for Unused Drugs
P
Law Enforcement Costs
P
Other Programs to Care for Addicted Patients and Their Families
Remedies sought will also include claims for injunctive relief to stop illicit opioids from entering
your communities and harming your constituents. For those communities impacted by the sober living
homes that have contributed to the crisis, the litigation can also address such concerns.
In taking on the multi-billion dollar pharmaceutical industry, it will take the strength of law firms
with decades of experience and success litigating against Big Pharma, and which are dedicated to
representing cities and counties nationwide in the opioid litigation. The Skikos and 0dm law firms in
combination have considerable experience in pharmaceutical drug litigation, and the Skikos firm
lawyers have been appointed in national and statewide pharmaceutical litigations and have litigated
those cases against Big Pharma to a successful resolution. In January 2018, Steve Skikos was appointed
by the federal district judge in Cleveland to be Plaintiffs’ Co-Liaison Counsel for the federal MDL opioid
litigation, where he will play a major role in the strategy and management of the litigation.

There is little risk to the counties and cities to participate in the litigation. All financial risk will be
absorbed by the consortium law firms which work on a contingency fee basis. The counties and cities
will pay nothing to file and maintain their actions unless and until there is a recovery.
The Role of Opioid Manufacturers and Distributors in the Epidemic
Opioids are derived from the poppy plant or are man-made in the lab. They range from natural
opioids such as opium and morphine, to semi-synthetic such as oxycodone, to fully synthetic such as
fentanyl, which is 50 to 100 times stronger than morphine.
Opioids create artificial endorphins in the brain, which bind to the brain’s opioid receptors producing
euphoric effects and providing pain relief. For many patients, opioids trick the brain into stopping
production of endorphins. When this happens, users experience excruciating withdrawal symptoms
thus leading to addiction. And when addicted patients can no longer get an opioid prescription, they
turn to other means to obtain opioids including turning to black market prescription opioids and heroin.
This cycle leads to a cascade of costs not only to the addicted individuals and their families, but also to
the cities and counties for emergency response, treatment law enforcement homelessness and other
costs.
The primary culprits who set the opioid crisis in motion are the manufacturers and distributors of
prescription opiates. These powerful and highly addictive drugs were approved by the FDA for limited
uses, such as for cancer pain, end of life treatment and as a last resort for pain therapy. But once
approved, the manufacturers embarked on a massive and illegal marketing campaign aimed at
expanding their use to be a first line of pain therapy and assuring doctors by downplaying their addiction
risk. The distributors, for their part, were required by law to monitor sales and to report to the Drug
Enforcement Agency any abnormal or suspicious changes in distribution. But the ‘Big 3”—McKesson,
AmerisourceBergen, and Cardinal Health—which control 85 percent of the distribution market, failed
to do so. Their tracking and reporting failures hid the true scope of the opioid abuses from authorities
and allowed the epidemic to explode.
The deliberate nature of the pharmaceutical industry’s conduct is documented in the 60 Minutes
telecast entitled, “The Whistleblower,” which aired in October 2017. The joint CBS and Washington
Post investigation of the distribution industry describes the manner in which the distributors turned a
blind eye to pain pills being diverted to illicit use. 60 minutes followed up with “Too Big to Prosecute,”
which aired in December 2017.
The Role of Litigation in Addressing the Epidemic
What started as a trickle of lawsuits by counties and cities has now progressed to a steady tide, as
hundreds of lawsuits have been filed nationally against the prescription opioid manufacturers and
distributors. In December 2017, a federal judicial panel ordered that all actions filed in federal courts
across the country be coordinated in a single proceeding called a Multi-District Litigation (or “MDL”) in
Cleveland, Ohio. The judge appointed to oversee the litigation, the Hon. Dan Polster, is a highly
experienced judge with prior success in running another pharmaceutical MDL involving nearly 1000
cases (In re Gadolinium, MDL 1909). Judge Polster recognized that the courts may be the best venue
for solving the opioid crisis, and already he set in motion discussions aimed towards early resolution of
the cases.
In addition, there are a number of state court litigations against the opioid manufacturers and
distributors. For instance, California and Pennsylvania are the homes to several of the manufacturers
and distributors, and so it is anticipated those and other state courts will be handling numerous cases
filed by counties, cities and other entities as well.
The filed cases include claims for Public Nuisance, which seeks injunctive relief and funding to abate
the opioid nuisance created by the manufacturers and distributors. They also include claims for

negligence and related common law claims to recoup prior costs associated with the epidemic. They
further assert claims for unfair competition, false advertising and other state and federal statutory
claims for injunctive relief, civil penalties and other remedies against the manufacturer and distributor
defendants for their unlawful conduct.
In short, the goal of litigation for counties and cities is to ensure they have an action on file and a
seat at the table when it comes time for resolution of the cases, so they can have a voice and be in a
position to maximize their recovery of funds in the litigation. It is the intent of any resolution that funds
go directly to the counties and cities so their elected officials can direct and control where those funds
are most needed to combat the epidemic in their communities. Without the clout of actual, active cases
on file in their own names, counties and cities are powerless and must rely on others to negotiate a
resolution and for funds to trickle to them.
Our Legal Team
Our law firms have the resources and expertise in pharmaceutical drug and complex litigation to
maximize recovery for our clients in the opioid litigation. Our lawyers, with the support of their peers,
have been appointed to leadership positions by dozens of courts in state and federal pharmaceutical
drug coordinated litigations. We have litigated successfully against many of the largest companies
involved in this litigation, including Johnson & Johnson, Teva Pharmaceuticals and McKesson. We have
access to experts in all fields touching this litigation who will assist us in evaluating the case and guiding
our litigation strategy, and, if necessary, testify at trial. We also have considerable experience in
negotiating and resolving these highly complex litigations to maximize recovery for our clients, and to
take them to trial if they are not willing to resolve litigations fairly and for adequate compensation.
Attached is further information about our law firms. If you would like further information or have
questions about the litigation or any potential representation, please do not hesitate to contact any
of the lawyers below and we would be happy to set up a call or meeting.
