HEYENNE
nway
RE:
MISCELLANEOUS GREENWAY DESIGN SEGMENTS
SCOPE OF WORK
TO:
Larry Gallagher, Summit Engineering
BY:
Richard Zita, Greenway/ Park Project Manager
DATE:
December 5, 2018
The City of Cheyenne will review design proposals for six segments along the Greenway Trail.
Five segments will be designed to 100% bid ready packages. The sixth will be a 35% package for
Union Pacific preliminary review. Funding will be from the 2017 6th Penny Tax. The segments
are described below:
1.
Powderhouse (between Gardenia and Storey Blvd.)
2.
South Park (between Partoyan to Waltershied; Waltershied to GW bridge on Deming)
3.
Henderson Drive (from E. Nationway to existing Greenway)
4.
Downtown Connector (MLK park across Lincoinway to Dey to Ames to Pump House park)
5.
Cowboy Dodge (from Advance Auto to Fat Boys driveway)
6.
Pump House Park f;5th St via UPRR underpass at Reed Ave. right-of-way)
Design fee proposals must be from professional engineering firms licensed to work in Wyoming
and must have a Statement of Qualifications (SOQ) on-file with the City Engineer’s office to be
considered. All drawings necessary for project completion will be completed by civil engineers
licensed by the Wyoming State Board of Examining Engineers.
Information submitted shall be incorporated by reference into and be considered part of the
contract between the City of Cheyenne and the selected firm. The City of Cheyenne reserves the
right to make modifications to the Scope of Work as it sees fit. Firms obtaining this Scope of
Work will be formally notified of any modifications.
A pre-submittal meeting will be scheduled at the nearest opportunity. An on-site walk through
will only be held upon request. Submittal of proposals, (1 hard copy, 1 digital format), are to be
filed with the Urban Planning Department, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Rm. 205, Cheyenne, WY by 2:00
pm. Friday, December 21, 2018.
Proiect Background:
The six trail segments were identified as high priority projects by public surveys, workshops,
meetings and members of the Greenway Advisory Committee. These segments are located
2019 6th Penny Greenway Design Segments
— Scope of Work Statement

December 5, 2018
Page 2
throughout the city and will help close gaps in the bike/ped system. Most segments are
traditional Greenway segments, with 10’ wide separated concrete trails, others are 8’ wide curb-
tight Shared Use trails along established roadways. One underpass is proposed below a single
track spur of the Union Pacific Railroad in downtown Cheyenne (Segment No. 6).
Two of the six segments still require access easements and the City is actively pursuing them.
In
lieu of accessing those sites to gather topographical information, the City will accept existing GIS
topographic information available from Laramie County or other recognized sources. Contractor
will not be responsible for securing or documenting needed access easements to complete their
design work.
Impervious surface areas greater than 20,000 s.f. will require coordination and approval from the
city’s storm water / water quality department.
Exhibits illustrating the desired alignments and connections are for reference only. The
contractor is advised to visit each site individually to verify existing conditions prior to submitting
a fee proposal. All design segments to meet current City and Greenway design standards,
including ADA-compliant curb cuts, transitions and tactile warning at designated approaches and
intersections.
The city would like to approve, bid and construct as many segments as possible during the 2019
building season.
Projects not completed in 2019 will be completed in 2020, provided funding is
available.
Scope of Work:
The selected firm will be asked to provide the following services:
a.
Verify all ROW and utility locations that may affect design work for the six
described trail segments. Notify PM of discrepancies or potential conflicts.
b.
Prepare plans and specifications for contractor bidding, including 35%, 65%,
95% and 100% design submittal packages.
U.
Include cost estimates with each design phase submittal. Provide hourly rates
to assist with bid preparation and include construction observation services for
the segments to be built. These hours can be negotiated at time of bidding.
e.
Provide all materials, equipment, tools and labor to complete the design work
described on the attached exhibits.
f.
The Contractor shall work with the Greenway project manager and the City’s
engineering department to prepare the design documents for construction
bidding.
g.
Attend council and engineering review meetings as required.
Pro iect Segments:
1.
Powderhouse C 100% design):
This shared trail segment is approximately 1,025’ long and connects Gardenia
2019 6th Penny Greenway Design Segments
— Scope of Work Statement

December 5, 2018
Page 3
south to the existing shared trail segment north of Storey. Design to connect the
existing segments in Gardenia to the intersection at Powderhouse.
Improvements include: an 8’ curb tight shared concrete path with gutter and
curb; a revised drainage swale or pipe as needed to handle surface flow; ADA
improvements at intersection with Gardenia and a marked cross walk.
Powderhouse looking north with unimproved edge
Powerhouse at existing shared walk terminus, just north of Storey, looking south
2019
6th Penny Greenway Design Segments
— Scope of Work Statement

December 5, 2018
Page 4
2.
South Park (100% design):
This segment is nearly 2,275’ and begins where the Partoyan Greenway
terminates at 6th Street, follows the 2012 conceptual plan by Alta, including a
bench and look-out point, traverses the slope down to Waltershied at no more
than a 5% grade; creates a new cross-walk and extends the GW along Deming to
intersect the existing Greenway at Crow Creek.
Fourth Street ROW at Thomes Street looking uphill to the west
2019 6th Penny Greenway Design Segments
— Scope of Work Statement

December 5, 2018
Page 5
3.
Henderson Drive
— South from Sparks Rd to existing Greenway (100% design):
This Greenway segment runs approximately 3,180’ from Sparks Road south
along the Henderson ditch to meet the existing Greenway in Sun Valley. Design
to connect to the existing footbridge leading to Lebhart School and provide a
timber guardrail the entire length along the Henderson ditch. Provide smooth
transitions at each end of new segment to existing grade. Provide stop and
safety signs per spec and cross walks as needed.
View from existing GW spur looking north along Henderson Ditch
•
------..
Henderson Ditch looking south. Verify gas line location and depth
2019
6th Penny Greenway Design Segments
— Scope of Work Statement

December 5, 2018
Page 6
4.
Downtown Connector (100% design):
This segment improves the cross-walk at Missile Drive and W. Lincolnway with
new ADA curbs, improved cross-walk striping, cross-walk signs and activators.
Segment utilizes the existing bridge along Lincolnway. Plan reconfigures Dey
Avenue into a one-way connector to Ames and utilizes the remaining lane for a
landscape bioswale buffer and Greenway. Dey Avenue realigns to meet Ames
Avenue at a ninety-degrees with new stop sign. Greenway makes turn onto
Ames and aligns with existing entry into Pump House Park. New cross-walks,
ADA ramps, in-pavement lights, refuge island and activators are included.
Existing MLK Park crossing at ‘
. Lincolnway
L3
1
Dey Avenue from Ames looking west
2019 6th Penny Greenway Design Segments — Scope of Work Statement

December 5, 2018
Page 7
5.
Cowboy Dodge between Advanced Auto and Fat Boys (100% design):
This 360’ x 8’ wide shared segment begins at 1109 E. Lincoinway, at the existing
east boundary of Advanced Auto Parts, and extends to the existing driveway at
Fat Boys, 1151 E. Lincolnway. Trail will need a landscape buffet along E.
Lincoinway as space allows. Trail may need a low retaining wall to level grades,
timber guardrail, and a continuous underground storm drain pipe per WYDOT
spec. within ROW. Stops signs at existing driveways crossings and marked cross
walks will be required. Access easement has not yet been secured. Plans to be
formatted for WYDOT review.
East edge at Fat Boys Driveway
f Cowboy Dodge at walk termination

6.
Pump House Park to 15th Street via UP Underpass 135% design):
This segment is approximately 585’ long and extends east from the ADA parking
pad in Pump House Park to 15th Street and includes an underpass section of
approximately 40’ below a single spur line of the UP Railroad. Design program
includes: 10’ wide Greenway with timber guardrail along both sides; conceptual
design for underpass box culvert, including lighting and typical section;
conceptual grading plan to insure positive drainage and transition at ;5th Street.
Survey and geotechnical report is included. Plans to be formatted for UP Railroad
review. City to prepare application.

03/2017 
 
City Contract # __________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
 
Landfill Leachate Pump Maintenance 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 2019, by 
and between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Terracon Consultants, located at 1505 Old Happy Jack Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
82001. (“Consultant”). 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibits A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed within eighteen (18) months 
from signature of the Agreement. All work under this Agreement will be completed by 
December 31, 2020.  
 
3. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the 
same or similar locality. Upon notice to the Consultant and by mutual agreement between 
the parties, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not 
meeting such a standard. 
 
4. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
5. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers and other consultants 
By Alessandra McCoy at 9:17 am, Jan 24, 2019

2 
 
under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are 
required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance 
Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the 
Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the 
responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this 
policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure 
their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in 
violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
6. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term 
or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by 
the Consultant, or its employees or sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  
The Consultant acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant 
to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
7. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or 
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Business Automobile Insurance 
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily 
injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per accident for the entire term of the 
Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the 
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services 
under this Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ 
compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 

3 
 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a. Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the 
City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect at the time the Consultant begins the provision of services under this 
Agreement; and 
b. In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new 
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect 
during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services under this 
Agreement; and 
c. Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this Agreement; 
and 
d. In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior 
to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this 
paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance written notification of 
the termination of said coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for 
an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period 
of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has 
provided services under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional 
liability insurance policies. 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the 
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that 
has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of 
insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional 
insured.  The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates 
shall not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a 
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
8. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES  49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 

4 
 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin or sex. 
 
9. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of Thirty-
Six Thousand Dollars ($36,000.00) as stated in Exhibit A.  
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written 
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and 
work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any 
payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts 
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default remains 
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
a. Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the 
contract amount; or 
b. Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
This Agreement shall be for a period of no longer than eighteen months.  The parties may 
by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon 

5 
 
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
a. Discontinue all services affected; and 
b. Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, reports, 
estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as may have been 
accumulated by the Consultant in performing this Agreement, whether completed 
or in process. 
c. In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant for 
accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such 
acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for 
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retain all immunities and defenses available 
to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and 
all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement 
actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental 
immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly 
construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental 
or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity.  
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts 
and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to 
examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; 
and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 

6 
 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
21. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that 
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
22. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of six (6) pages, and Exhibit A consisting of nine (9) pages, 
contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, 
written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.  
 
 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have hereunto set their hands and seals the day and year 
first above written. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City of Cheyenne

(SEAL) 
 
 
                        
Marian J. Orr, Mayor

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING
Date:
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THE LARAMIE CO’
CONSERVATION DISTRICT, ROTARY CLUBS OF CHEYENNE,
AND FROG CREEK PARTNERS, LLC
1.
Parties. This Memorandum of Understanding (“MOU”) is made and entered into by and
between the City ofCheyenne, Wyoming, a municipal corporation (the “City”), the Laramie County
Conservation District, a Wyoming Conservation District (“LCCD”), Rotary Clubs of Cheyenne
(“Rotary”), and Frog Creek Partners, LLC and its agents (“Frog Creek Partners”). The City, the
LCCD, Rotary and Frog Creek Partners are, at times, individually referred to as a “Party” and
collectively referred to as the “Parties.” The Parties’ addresses are set forth in Section 8(i) below.
2.
Purpose. Stormwater pollution is directly affecting water quality in Crow Creek, a perennial
stream listed for sediment and F. coli on the Wyoming Department of Environment Quality
(WDEQ) 303(d) impaired streams list. The LCCD and Rotary have purchased twelve (12) retrofit
Gutter Bin filtration systems (“Gutter Bins”) from Frog Creek Partners with a plan to install them
downtown in the Capitol Basin (the “Property” as shown in Exhibit A). The purpose of this MOU
is to set forth the City’s terms and conditions by which the LCCD, Rotary and Frog Creek Partners
may conduct installation and maintenance of the Gutter Bins (the “Project”).
3.
Term of MOU. This MOU shall commence upon the date last signed and executed by the
duly authorized representatives of the Parties to this MOU and shall remain in full force and effect
until the Project is completed, unless terminated earlier pursuant to Section 8(b) below.
4.
Payment. No payment shall be made by any Party to any other Party as a result of this
MOU.
5.
Responsibilities of the City.
a.
Subject to the conditions set forth in this MOU, the City permits the LCCD, Rotary
and Frog Creek Partners to enter upon the Property subject to this MOU for purposes ofconducting
installation and maintenance of the Project.
b.
The City agrees to consult with the LCCD, Rotary and Frog Creek Partners on
matters relating to the Project.
c.
The City must approve any and all final design plans relating to the Project.
d.
The City will clean all stormwater catch basins in which Gutter Bins are to be
installed prior to installation and on a schedule to be mutually agreed upon and weather permitting.
Cleaning of catch basins shall not unreasonably delay the Project.
e.
The City will accept captured pollutants from the Project at the City of Cheyenne
Landfill located at 1461 Happy Jack Road at no cost to the other Parties. The pollutants will meet
federal and state regulation for disposal and comply with Wyoming Department of Environmental
Quality Happy Jack Landfill Lifetime Permit SHWD File #10.080.
Page 1 of 5

f.
The City will not unreasonably deny expansion of the Project if requested by the
other parties to this MOU.
g.
The City has no other responsibilities relating to the Project, including any financial
responsibilities.
6.
Responsibilities of LCCD.
a.
LCCD agrees to provide the City with a proposed installation and maintenance
schedule prior to engaging in any work on the Property, including any change in location of Gutter
Bins.
b.
LCCD may employ or use the services of Frog Creek Partners on the Project. The
LCCD agrees to require Frog Creek Partners to provide proofofthe following insurance coverages:
i.
Commercial General Liability Insurance: For claims arising out of bodily
injury, illness or death, or from damage to or destruction ofproperty ofothers, including loss
or use thereof, with minimum limits of$ 1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for
the entire term of the MOU.
ii.
Business Automobile Insurance: Including owned, non-owned and hired
vehicles with minimum limits for bodily injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per
accident for the entire term of the MOU.
iii.
Any and all non-City employees of involved in the Project or other persons
while engaged in the performance of any work or services required by the Project shall not
be considered employees of the City, and any and all claims that may arise under the
Workman’s Compensation Act on behalf of said employees or other persons while so
engaged shall be the sole obligation and responsibility of the employing party.
c.
LCCD accepts responsibility for the actions ofits employees, Rotary and Frog Creek
Partners on the Project and agrees to indenmi& and defend the City against any and all claims
against the City arising out of the actions of these parties while engaged in any work relating to this
MOU.
d.
LCCD agrees to invite the City to all Project meetings.
e.
Prior to installation of said Gutter Bins, LCCD agrees to obtain approval from the
City on Project limits and maintenance.
f.
All final sizing, installation and maintenance plans must conform to the applicable
permitting, regulatory, or other legal requirements of the Wyoming Department of Environmental
Quality.
Page 2 of 5

7.
Suecial Provisions.
a.
The City reserves the right to monitor all activities related to this MOU that are
performed by the LCCD, Rotary, and Frog Creek Partners. This shall include, but not be limited to,
the right to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to examine books, ledgers,
documents, papers, and records pertaining to this MOU; and, to observe personnel in every phase
of performance of the related work.
b.
The Parties agree that the LCCD, Rotary, or Frog Creek Partners at no time will take
ownership ofthe captured pollutants in the servicing ofthe Gutter Bins. The pollutants will remain
the property of the City.
c.
The Parties acknowledge that the purchase ofthe Gullet Bins is subject to Frog Creek
Partners’ general terms and conditions.
8.
General Provisions.
a.
Amendments. This MOU may be amended only in writing and upon mutual consent
ofthe Parties. Any changes, modifications, revisions or amendments that are mutually agreed upon
shall be incorporated by written instrument, executed, and signed by the Parties.
b.
Termination. This MOU may be terminated, without cause, by any Party upon
fifteen (15) days written notice, which notice shall be delivered by hand or by certified mail pursuant
to the procedures set forth in Section 8(i) below.
c.
Applicable LawNenue. The constmction, interpretation, and enforcement of this
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming
shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of this MOU and over the Parties, and the venue
shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie Counw. Wyoming.
d.
Entirety of MOU.
This MOU represents the entire and integrated agreement
between the Parties on the subject matter of the MOU and supersedes all prior negotiations,
representations and agreements, whether written or oral.
e.
Prior Approval. This MOU shall not be binding upon any Party unless this MOU
has been reduced to writing before performance begins as described under the terms of this MOU,
and unless this MOU is approved as to form by the City Attorney of Cheyenne, Wyoming or her
representative.
f.
Severability. Should any portion ofthis MOU be judicially determined to be illegal
or unenforceable, the remainder of the MOU shall continue in fiJI force and effect.
g.
Governmental/Sovereign Immunity.
The City and LCCD do not waive their
governmental or sovereign immunities by entering into this MOU, including, but not limited to,
those immunities provided by Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, el seq., and hilly retain all immunities and
defenses provided by law with respect to any action based on or occurring as a result ofthis MOU.
Page 3 of 5

h.
Indemnification. Except as identified in Section 6(c) above, each Party to this MOU
hereby acknowledges and agrees that it shall be responsible for its own acts arising out ofthis MOU.
No Party agrees to defend or indemniI’ another Party.
i.
Notices. Any notice or correspondence required to be given by the terms of this
MOU shall be delivered by hand, or delivered by certified mail, postage prepaid, to the address of
the respective Party below:

j.

k.
Assignment. No Party shall assign this MOU without prior written consent of all

1.
Third Party Rights. Nothing in this MOU shall be construed to give any rights or

m.

The above-referenced is modified as set forth in the following, and these amendments and
modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other aspects of the
original agreement remain unchanged and in fill! force and effect.
I.
Description of Changes.
The City of Cheyenne (“City”) and Asbestos & Technical Services (“Consultant”) entered
into a professional services agreement dated December 10,2018. That contract bears city contract
number 6932 (“Agreement”).
The Agreement contemplates completion of the scope of work
identified therein by February 28, 2019.
The parties agree additional time is needed to remove
asbestos containing materials from the residential property located at 2247 East I 9Ih Street,
Cheyenne, Wyoming.
II.
New Contract Items.
The new contract item identified here is additional time to complete the scope of work
identified in the Agreement.
Ill.

to December 31, 2019.

6932

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
for
Abatement Services
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this 10th
day of
December
, 2018, by
and between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001
(“City”), and Asbestos & Technical Services, located at 10990 Red Sky Loop, Cheyenne,
Wyoming 82009 (“Consultant”).
RECITALS
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and
between the parties as follows:
1.
SCOPE OF WORK
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference
and expressly made a part hereof.
2.
TIME OF PERFORMANCE
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by February 28, 2019.
3.
CONSULTANT RESPONSIIJTY
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the
same or similar locality. Upon notice to the Consultant and by mutual agreement between
the parties, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not
meeting such a standard.
4.
COMPLIANCE WiTh LAWS
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.
5.
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City of
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. MI architects, engineers and other consultants
under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are
required to comply with thp provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance
Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the
03/2017

Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the
responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this
policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure
their compliance, if the Coñsültant, theft employees or sub-consultants are found in
violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated.
6.
NDEMNWICATJONIHOLD HARMLESS
The Consultant agrees to indenmify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result
of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property,
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental
laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term
or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by
the Consultant, or its employees or sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.
The Consultant acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant
to the terms of this paragraph.
7.
INSURANCE REqUIREMENTS
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages:
Commercial General Liability Insurance
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement.
Workers’ Compensation
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services
under this Agreement.
Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’
compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage.
Professional Liability Insurance
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties
under this Agreement. The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.
if this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy:
a.
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the
City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be
in effect at the time the Consultant begins the provision of services under this
Agreement; and
b.
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new
2

certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect
during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services under this
Agreement; and
c.
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period offive (5) years
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this Agreement;
and
d.
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior
to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this
paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance written notification of
the termination of said coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for
an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period
of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has
provided services under this Agreement.
Additional Insurance Information
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by
endorsement on its insurance policies, and shall provide the City with a copy of the
endorsements. This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional
liability insurance policies.
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement.
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that
has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach
of this Agreement.
if requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of
insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional
insured. The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates
shall not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder.
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent).
8.
MNORHY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26
Mi parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in,
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national
origin or sex.
9.
COMPENSATION
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of Forty-
Two Thousand One Hundred Dollars ($42,100) as stated in Exhibit A.
3

10.
MONTHLY REPORT
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and
work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any
payment request until the report is submitted.
11.
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of
any kind on the behalf of the City.
12.
TAXES
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement.
13.
DEFAULT
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default.
14.
REMEDIES
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default. In the event the default remains
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to:
a.
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the
contract amount; or
b.
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance.
15.
TERM AND TERMINATION
This Agreement shall be for a period of three months. The parties may by mutual written
agreement renew or extend this Agreement.
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant,
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at anytime, for the City’s convenience. Upon
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall:
a.
Discontinue all services affected; and
b.
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, reports,
estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as may have been
accumulated by the Consultant in performing this Agreement, whether completed
or in process.
c.
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant for
accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such
acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld.
4

16.
ADDiTIONAL REMEDIES
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.
17.
GOVERNThIG LAW. JURISDICTION AND VENUE
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by
the laws of the State of Wyoming.
The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming.
18.
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNiTY
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the
Agreement. Furthermore, the City specifically retain all immunities and defenses available
to it asa sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, a seq., and
all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement
actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental
immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly
construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental
or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity.
19.
MONiTORING ACTIVITIES
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts
and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to
examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement;
and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work.
20.
NON-DISCRIMINATION
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat.
§ 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C.
§
12101, ci seq., and the Age
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto,
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color,
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection
with the performance under this Agreement.
21.
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION
5

The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary.
22.
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT
This Agreement consisting of six pages, and Exhibit A consisting of two pages, contains
the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, written or
oral, concerning or controlling this matter.
IN WiTNESS WHEREOF, the parties have hereunto set their hands and seals the day and year
first above written.

Flooring Abatement
Based on site visit and information provided by the owner, Asbestos & Technical Services (ATS) has
prepared the following proposal to remove asbestos containing materials from the residential property
located at 2247 E 19th Street, Cheyenne, WY. The identified asbestos materials will be removed as
pad of a demolition project.
Prolect Scope
The base scope of work includes removing the following:
- Asbestos textured drywall from walls and/or ceilings in the Family Room, Kitchen, Dining Room, Hall
Bathroom, Hallway, Bedroom, Workout Room, Powder Bathroom, Back Entrance and Associated
Closets of the main level/lower level of the home (excludes drywall in staircase or basement level)
- Asbestos spray-applied texture and drywall from ceilings in the Family Room, Hallway, Bedroom,
Dining Room and Workout Room areas of the main level) lower level of the home
- Asbestos vinyl floor tile and mastic in the Living Room, Kitchen, Bedroom, Hallway, Laundry Room
and Storage Room in the basement level of the home
- Asbestos vinyl floor tile and mastic in the Bonus Room that is accessible from the back yard and
beneath the building addition/first floor of the building
- Asbestos glazing by component removal of associated windows that are on the first floor and
basement level of the building
- Asbestos cement siding (“transite”) that is on all for exterior sides of the home
The asbestos materials will be removed within a series of containments or regulated work areas that
will created using 6 ml. polyethylene sheeting. All containment structures will be kept under continuous
ne9ative air flow throughout the abatement process. All waste material will be disposed of at an EPA-
approved facility.
Project Costs
The cost to complete the Base Scope detailed above is $39,700.00 and includes the inspection,
abatement labor, materials, equipment, tools, travel costs needed to perform the abatement scope and
waste disposal at an EPA-approved site.
Page 1 of 2

Alternate:
During the mobilization for the Base Scope, ATS will remove the old furnace that is in the
basement level of the building to account for suspect building materials that are present within this
furnace box. The price to remove the furnace is 52,400.00 and will be added to the price above.
Clarifications
ATS assumes that the owner will not provide adequate power and water needed to perform the
abatement work listed above. ATS will provide a fuel-powered generator to perform the work scope.
The furnace may need to run 24 hours a day until this project is complete.
Other asbestos material locations or quantities, types of asbestos-containing materials, or removal and
remediation of other types of hazardous materials are “unknown” and excluded from this proposal. ATS
will need to attach poly sheeting on walls in order to build an effective containment. While every effort
will be made to minimize paint damage when taking down the containment, it is not unusual for small
patches of paint damage to occur.
ATS has not made provision for spot, touch-up painting,
if
necessary, in its Base Scope proposal.
Thank you for considering ATS for this project.
Approved and accepted by*:
Asbestos & Technical Services

The above-referenced contract is modified as set forth in the following, and these
amendments and modifications of the pians and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All
other aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect.
L
Description of Changes
The City of Cheyenne (“City”) and River Oaks Communications Corporation (“River
Oaks”) entered into a professional services agreement dated October 24, 2017.
That contract
bears city contract number 6743 (“Agreement”). The Agreement, as amended effective April 23,
2018, contemplates completion of the scope of work identified therein by September 30, 2018.
But
additional
time
is
now
needed
for the
drafting and
negotiation
of the
Charter
Communications Cable Television Franchise Agreement.
U. New Contract Items
The new contract items identified here are additional time to complete the scope of work
identified in the Agreement and the drafting, development and finalization of new Wireless
Communication Facilities Regulations for Small Cells for the City.
ifi. Schedule
This contract modification extends the completion of service date from September 30,
2018 to February 28, 2019.
